# Findings register

Every finding gets a stable ID for life. Never delete a row, never let a finding disappear into a
replacement document — update its disposition in place. If a reviewer disagrees with a finding,
record `REJECTED WITH EVIDENCE` and the reasoning, never silence removal.

All findings below originate from `EXTERNAL-REVIEW-DECISION-2026-09-14.md`, the first review run
under this process. Individual finding files (`ER-20260914-F0NN.md`) hold full detail; this table
is the at-a-glance disposition.

| F044 | Medium (data-integrity — a stored value the runner and the coach both consumed is wrong, not just imprecise) | Today's run stored `avgCadence: 143` (stride 5) and `avgCadence: 133` (stride 6) on two 20-22 second stride segments, and the coach consultant built a real "compressed recovery costs him first" narrative on top of those numbers before anyone checked plausibility. **Confirmed wrong, not just suspect, by two independent lines of evidence.** (1) Data-quality: each stride phase carries only 4-6 total HR samples across the whole interval (~1 reading per 4-5 seconds) and there is no raw cadence-sample array in the run JSON at all — `avgCadence` is a single stored scalar with nothing behind it to verify against, unlike HR. (2) David's own direct, unprompted testimony, live in this session: "there is no way in hell I ran the last strides at 143 or 133 cadence" — his real range is ~155 (lowest, even fresh) to 165 (typical target) to ~170 (fast efforts); 130s "NEVER happens." He also confirmed effort felt equal across all 6 reps, route was his normal flat route with nothing unusual, and no fatigue/nutrition factor — ruling out the obvious alternative explanations for a real cadence collapse. **Root cause traced (best-supported, code-and-data-evidenced, honestly not 100% certain):** stride phases stream from the Watch app's `WorkoutTracker.swift`, where per-phase `avgCadence` time-weight-averages a raw, unsmoothed mirror of CoreMotion's `CMPedometer.currentCadence`, refreshed only when CoreMotion's own lag-prone callback fires. Critically, cadence has NO staleness watchdog, while HR and pace in the SAME file both do (`lastHrSampleAt`/`hrStaleAfterSec` zeroes a frozen HR reading after 20s) — on a multi-minute interval a few seconds of CoreMotion lag is negligible; on a ~20-22s stride it can be 25-50% of the whole window. Data confirms the shape: every "Walk back" recovery phase reads 113-127 spm; strides 1-4 read 160-165 (matches David's real range); strides 5/6 (143, 133) sit exactly between the walking and running bands — consistent with a phase-boundary lag artifact, not a real physiological collapse. The 5-second `paceSamples` telemetry (which IS persisted, unlike cadence) shows an identical ramp-up shape in ALL six strides including the good ones, since pace self-corrects as a true distance/time ratio while cadence's live-snapshot averaging doesn't — and rep 6's preceding recovery was also cut short (34s vs. the normal ~62-64s), consistent with a timing mismatch specific to that rep. Honestly could not fully confirm (no raw per-second cadence timeline is persisted anywhere to verify against, unlike HR/pace) — states plainly that a brief, unrelated CoreMotion gait-detection glitch confined to those two reps shares the identical code path and data footprint and cannot be ruled out. Fix shape identified, not implemented: (1) add a cadence staleness watchdog mirroring the existing HR pattern; (2) persist a per-phase cadence timeline mirroring `phaseHrSamples`/`phasePaceSamples` so a future case is verifiable rather than inferred. The "compressed recovery costs him first" framing remains retracted. | Open — root cause traced, fix identified but not implemented | — | `programme-internal-working/00-master-programme/F044-AVGCADENCE-SHORT-INTERVAL-2026-09-14.md` |

| F050 | High (real, LIVE, currently-reachable defect on real production data — not hypothetical) | Today's run-consumption trace surfaced this live on the actual run being traced: the post-run recap and Today card both tell David "The plan moved after this run" — but the responsible `coach_intents` row (`vdot_auto_recalc`) fired 04:04:34 UTC, ~17 HOURS BEFORE this run finished (~20:57 UTC), from an unrelated same-calendar-day plan rebuild. Root cause, live-confirmed: (1) `postrun/load.ts`'s adaptation lookback is calendar-day granular (`ts >= dateISO::date AND ts < dateISO::date + 2 days`), not gated on the run's own clock time, so anything anywhere in the run's calendar day — including hours BEFORE it — gets swept in as "after this run"; (2) the clarifying-clause mechanism specifically built 2026-09-05 to prevent exactly this confusion only fires when `evidence.role === 'CORROBORATES'` — this run's role is `CONTEXT_ONLY`, so the clause silently skips, even though the underlying confusion is the identical shape it was built for; (3) independently, the `vdot_auto_recalc` row itself carries a genuinely empty `why` field. **Sharpened by external review, sharper mechanism than originally stated**: the calendar-day-granular lookback was deliberately widened on 2026-09-02 to avoid missing a late cron pass — a real tradeoff, not an oversight — but as a side effect it can't distinguish "before this run, same day" from "after this run." The clarifying sentence itself already EXISTS and is correct (`describeAdaptationCause('vdot_auto_recalc')` returns "your race result recalculated your fitness baseline directly") — it's just never reaching the runner because this event's role is `CONTEXT_ONLY`, not `CORROBORATES`. And the "empty why" isn't a blank string: `coach_intents.value` for this row is the bare string `"44.1"`, not a JSON object — `JSON.parse` gives a number, `.why` on a number is `undefined`, and the safe-why guard correctly returns null for a shape it was never built to see. **Confirmed NOT the same root cause as F038/F040/F047** — those are run-to-plan-workout matching across plan versions; this is event-to-run attribution via calendar-date proximity instead of real clock ordering. Same mistake family, different files, no shared fix. Fix shape: filter the lookback on the run's own `startUtc` instead of/alongside the calendar-day bound, and reconsider the `CORROBORATES`-only gate for reason codes like `vdot_auto_recalc` that carry a self-contained cause regardless of evidence role. Net effect unchanged: David reads a plan-change notice attached to an unrelated, earlier event, with zero explanation — live, right now. | Open — root cause fully traced and independently verified; fix not yet dispatched, queued behind tonight's higher-priority threads | — | `programme-internal-working/00-master-programme/RUN-CONSUMPTION-TRACE-2026-09-14-184425083906740.md` |
| F049 | High (real, precisely-scoped gap — confirmed by direct code read, not inferred) | Coach consultant investigation: an easy run's HR-ceiling verdict (`easy-discipline.ts`) compares only the run's AVERAGE HR against `hr_cap_bpm` — today's run averaged 142 against a 151 cap and was marked "hit" despite 41 real seconds above the cap late in the phase (see F044/HR-ceiling debrief). A genuine drift-aware mechanism does exist (`hr-thirds.ts`, splits a run into early/middle/late thirds, flags real drift >8bpm) but it's wired only into the weekly adaptation/progression pipeline, not into the per-run easy-discipline check — so a single run's own "did I stay in the zone" verdict never sees it. Even where the thirds mechanism does run, it works on per-mile HR averages, not raw ~5-second samples; the codebase's own comment in `activity-evidence.ts` names this exact limitation unprompted ("may never look like an interruption" for a short excursion inside a mile) and calls the finer-grained fix "a future pass" — i.e. openly not yet built, not something dug out by inference. Today's run is a clean, real, live example of exactly what this gap predicts. Fix shape (wire finer samples into `easy-discipline.ts`, or build a separate simple "N consecutive seconds over cap" check for easy runs) is an implementation call, not yet made. **Addendum (design review, finding 005): a cheaper, more-accurate fix may already be sitting live on the watch — `WorkoutEngine.swift`'s `hrOverCeiling` flag checks live HR against the ceiling every tick, correctly, honestly (no sticky false-positives), but is pure ephemeral UI state that's discarded every run instead of persisted. Persisting its transitions (or a running over-ceiling-seconds counter) into the run payload may be simpler than reconstructing drift from mile-level or 5-second samples after the fact. Source-traced, not directly observed firing on today's run (circumstantially corroborated via the run's own maxHr/hrZonePcts/absence of a ceilingLift field).** | Open — not yet dispatched; fix approach may be simpler than originally scoped, per design review's addendum | — | `for coaching consult/consult-log/2026-09-14-010-hr-drift-detection-gap.md`; `for design review/findings/2026-09-14-005-watch-already-knows-the-hr-ceiling-breach-live-and-throws-it-away.md` |

| F056 | High (real, precisely traced, David hit this live on his own phone — real priority) | A completed run doesn't appear on Today until force-quit/relaunch. Root cause traced precisely by external review: `FaffApp.swift` posts `.faffForegroundRefresh` twice on foreground (immediately, then again after `HealthKitImporter.importIfConnected` finishes — specifically to catch a just-landed run). `SurfaceStoreV5.swift`'s `ForegroundWork.shouldLoadOnForeground` coalesces the two into one `load()` if they land within 3s of each other (`foregroundLoadCoalesceSec`), measured from when the FIRST load STARTS, not when it finishes. If the HealthKit import completes in under 3s (plausible — REQUESTSTORM-2's own notes cite comparable 0.7-2s round-trips), the second, load-bearing post gets silently swallowed and the first load already ran on stale "no run yet" data — nothing re-fetches after that. Force-quit resets `lastForegroundLoadAt` to `.distantPast`, which is why that "fixes" it and a plain foreground doesn't. **Third instance of a related bug family in this exact code** (original single-throttle bug, then REQUESTSTORM-2) — each fix moved the throttle without re-examining whether the coalescing window itself could swallow a load-bearing second post. Fix direction: the window should originate from load-FINISH (or the import's own completion), not load-start — or the second post should bypass coalescing entirely when it's specifically the post-import one. Unconfirmed, worth ruling in/out before calling a fix complete: `WatchSync.swift`'s own completion-relay queue (~line 208-229) has an independent 60s throttle that may not reliably trigger `.faffForegroundRefresh` when it drains — a second possible route to the same symptom if David was wearing the Watch. **Fix landed.** Bypass mechanism chosen over the window-edge fix (confirmed the window-edge approach would have made it WORSE — the HealthKit import and the surface's own GET run as concurrent tasks, so a finish-stamped throttle produces a SMALLER gap than start-stamped on ordinary timing). WatchSync path traced: the literal 60s-throttle hypothesis was NOT confirmed (real delivery callbacks bypass it directly), but a real, independently-reachable second route to the identical symptom WAS found (`flushPendingCompletions()`'s own post shares the same notification/window) and fixed with the same mechanism. Verified via a real integration test against the actual NotificationCenter/observer code path on a real booted Simulator, falsified per Rule 18 (reverted, reproduced the exact symptom, restored, confirmed green). | **CLOSED — SHIPPED.** Standing merge authorization confirmed real via David's Desk, directly from David, 2026-09-15 (CLAUDE.md, locked). Composed at the exact reviewed SHA (`53fdbb21d`), rebased cleanly onto current main (no conflicts, content byte-identical, verified by direct diff), full `FaffTests` suite re-run twice clean (552/552, uncontended) before push. Fast-forwarded onto `main` at `2d9c0cae865ecf31393ecf5886ec16554af1fb2a`. **Live in TestFlight build 294**, confirmed via App Store Connect directly (VALID/IN_BETA_TESTING), not just merged. **SHIPPED — TestFlight build 294, confirmed VALID/IN_BETA_TESTING in App Store Connect (not just script success), 2026-09-15.** | `2d9c0cae865ecf31393ecf5886ec16554af1fb2a` | `programme-internal-working/00-master-programme/F056-FOREGROUND-REFRESH-COALESCING-2026-09-14.md` |

| F053 | Low-Medium (real lexicon gap, confirmed on BOTH iOS and web, found via proactive coach-voice audit) | `HostsV5.swift` (iOS) has five distinct error cases (offline, timeout, server error, cancelled, unknown) that all end in the bare phrase "Try again." with no qualifier — "software with nothing to say," exactly the failure mode the coach-voice lexicon's guard 5 exists to catch. **Confirmed NOT iOS-only**: `components/faff-app/views/TodayView.tsx:5438` has the identical bare-fallback shape ("Could not save. Try again."). The lexicon bans "please try again" and "try again in a moment" but not the bare phrase itself — a substring-matching gap, not a missing rule, and a per-string iOS-only patch would leave the same problem live on web. Also confirmed clean and NOT a false-positive risk: `'That did not save. Nothing was written, so it is safe to try again.'` (9 files) explains what happened and earns its "try again" — do not bundle that pattern into this fix. Fix direction: patch the lexicon itself (ban bare "try again", or flag any short string ending in it with nothing substantive before it) so both platforms are caught by one rule, plus fix the specific five iOS strings and the one web string to actually say what happened and reassure training data is safe. **Scope expanded further, real, not just more sites**: `HostsV5.swift`'s settings-error type has a SECOND string table with the identical defect — a sibling `shortCause` property (same fact "said as a clause rather than a whole screen," per its own comment, used for inline partial-load banners), 5 of 6 cases also ending in bare "Try again." A fix touching only the already-reported `message` property leaves `shortCause` broken — check for this sibling wherever the fix lands. Two more independently confirmed sites, same shape: `RPEV5.swift` (2 call sites) and `BlockV5.swift`. One near-hit correctly ruled out by reading context rather than trusting a grep match: `DecisionsSectionV5.swift`'s apparent bare "Try again." is actually part of a real two-line good-pattern string. | Open — not yet dispatched; full confirmed scope: HostsV5 (2 tables × 5 cases), TodayView.tsx, RPEV5 (×2), BlockV5 — separately track F079 (different root cause) | — | `for coaching consult/consult-log/2026-09-14-013-proactive-voice-audit-01.md` |

The Sep 8 gradient-discontinuity fix (`DesignV5/PanelV5.swift`, 3-pass smoothing kernel over 65 stops) was tuned and verified against the Easy/rest/phase/long ramp's middle-stop geometry (76%) — but Quality and Race panels use a DIFFERENT middle-stop location (52%, a deliberate David-ruled choice for text-clarity reasons, not itself a bug) via `Theme.V5.DayState.darkInkLocations`. The Swift file's own Xcode preview data uses the correct `'race'` value, so anyone previewing in Xcode has seen it right while the shipped app shows it wrong — likely why it went uncaught visually. | F061 | High (real, directly observed BOTH states on the same account, no "unconfirmed" caveat needed) | Shoes screen silently shows zero shoes on a failed fetch, with no error/retry UI at all — design review directly observed both states minutes apart on David's real account, no code change between them: first load showed empty "IN ROTATION" straight to "Add a pair"; second load rendered all 5 active shoes correctly. Independently confirmed via `GET /api/shoe` that David has 8 real shoes (5 active, 3 retired) the whole time — the data was never gone. Root cause, read directly: `ShoesHostV5.load()` in `HostsV5.swift` is `shoes = (try? await API.fetchShoes())?.shoes ?? []` — ANY failure (network blip, timeout, decode error) silently becomes "you own zero shoes," with no error state, no retry, nothing. Textbook Rule 11 (a failed read must never present as a real empty state). The fix pattern already exists ~200 lines away in the same file — `SettingsLoadFailure`, added 2026-09-07 for exactly this reason, per David's own quoted instruction at the time: "Do not swallow the error or replace every failure with 'Can't reach faff.'" Whatever specifically triggered this instance (design review's local test server was slow) is incidental — the code has no failure path other than silent-empty regardless of cause. **Re-confirmed independently, 2026-09-15** (originally logged as its own new finding F136, folded in here on recognizing the duplicate — see F136's row for the note): same bug, same symptom (empty "IN ROTATION," no error banner) hit again tonight via a different trigger (this session's walk-substrate harness issue on `/api/shoe`, see F134 — a different root cause for the same underlying missing-failure-path defect), and the same real shoe (NB SC Trainer v3) confirmed present in the account both times. Two independent sightings now, two different triggers, one unfixed code path. | Open — not yet dispatched, existing fix pattern in the same file to reuse. Now hit twice tonight by two different triggers (a slow test server, then a harness-level route failure) — same unfixed `try? ... ?? []` collapse both times. **Implemented, submitted for external review** (`RR-20260914-048`, confirmed VALID by the real validator after `--preflight`). The cited fix pattern was correct but incomplete: `API.fetchShoes()` itself silently `return nil` on a bad HTTP status without throwing, unlike its sibling `createShoe` -- meaning `SettingsLoadFailure`'s categorizer had nothing to categorize regardless of any view-layer fix. Fixed at the actual root (throws now, matching `createShoe`; confirmed safe for all 4 existing `try?`-based callers by reading each directly) plus the view-layer fix reusing `SettingsHostV5`'s exact 3-state pattern. 552/552 FaffTests pass. **CONFIRMED — `RR-20260914-048`, external review, no findings.** Every reuse claim independently checked against the actual surrounding code, not accepted from the manifest: `APIError.badStatus` and the throw pattern already exist (`createShoe`'s own sibling); grepped all 4 existing `fetchShoes()` callers, confirmed every one uses `try?` so the throw-vs-nil change is invisible to them; confirmed `SETTINGSFAIL-1`/`SettingsHostV5.loadFailure` genuinely pre-exist with the identical "never blank a screen that already has good content" posture this fix mirrors; confirmed `ErrorNote`/`Skeleton`/`AppBar` are real pre-existing shared components, not new inventions. Independently built and ran the full suite: 552/552, exact match. **Went beyond the manifest's own honest disclosure that no dedicated test exists for this UI-state fix — demonstrated it directly rather than accepting it at face value**: reverted the actual regression this finding fixes, reran the full suite, still passed 552/552 with the bug back in place, genuinely proving the disclosed coverage gap is real (the corpus can't reach that code path), not an excuse covering something worse. Same Rule 13 render-verification gap as F132/RR-046, same honest reason (credential-entry constraint, correctly not crossed). Fully checked, fixed, and independently verified — qualifies for the standing merge authorization. **MERGED to `main` 2026-09-15** (`34a5c3e45f19`). Deploy status not yet confirmed at merge time (Railway BUILDING). | `34a5c3e45f19` | `for design review/findings/2026-09-14-008`; re-confirmed `for design review/SETTINGS-AUDIT-2026-09-14.md` item 5; `for external review/review-results/RR-20260914-048/review.json`, 2026-09-15 |

But `BlockV5.swift`'s panel unconditionally does `FaffValueText(model.panel.dose.unreadableIfAbsent, ...)`, and `unreadableIfAbsent` maps nil to `FaffValue.unreadable`, rendered with the fault color — a nil value that should draw nothing at all is instead shown as a visible error indicator to every user, every time. The correct pattern already exists in the same codebase: `TodayBeforeV5`'s shared `HeroDayPanelContentV5` component handles this exact shape correctly (`if let dose {...}`, draws nothing when absent), with its own header comment naming precisely this case. `BlockV5` duplicates the hero-panel layout inline instead of reusing that shared component, and the duplication is where it diverged. **Follow-up confirmed the other 3 `unreadableIfAbsent` call sites are clean, not a cluster**: `RaceDetailV5.swift`'s 3 sites are already correctly guarded (`showsColumns()` gates on `isEmpty==false` before `unreadableIfAbsent` ever runs — same fix already landed there previously, with its own comment "AN UNSET GOAL IS NOT AN UNREADABLE ONE"); `RacesV5.swift`'s 1 site is lower-risk by construction (dose only null in a case that doesn't occur on real data). F062 is confirmed isolated to Block. | Open — not yet dispatched, existing correct pattern in the same codebase to reuse; confirmed isolated to Block, no other call sites affected; fix as a normal correctness bug at normal priority | — | `for design review/findings/2026-09-14-009` |

| F063 | High if real, unconfirmed (source-traced only, honestly disclosed) — could affect a brand-new runner's literal first screen | Same `unreadableIfAbsent` bug class as F062 may reach `OnboardingHostV5.submit`'s day-one reveal poster: it runs the identical `today.panel.dose.unreadableIfAbsent` conversion, and `OnboardingRevealPanel` draws it with no guard, same unguarded shape as Block. If a new runner's day one happens to land on a rest day (not confirmed impossible), their very first screen after finishing signup shows a stray fault dash instead of their prescription. Design review traced this from source only — explicitly NOT render-confirmed, since reaching it needs a genuinely fresh signup state not available against the walk-substrate this pass. **Reachability confirmed real, not moot**: design review read `lib/plan/generate.ts:8445`'s "get them running on day one" mechanism directly — it's a deliberate mitigation that relocates an easy run onto the anchor day when it would otherwise be rest, but its own header comment names two real no-op cases: a low-frequency week that's all long+quality with no easy day to relocate, and an anchor day unavailable per the runner's stated days with no earlier day available either. A low-frequency onboarder, or one whose available days don't align with the anchor, can genuinely land day one on rest. **Code agent's own investigation found a deeper, more general root cause than the two no-op cases above**: the safeguard's guard clause (`generate.ts:11539`) checks `input.startMondayISO`, but per WEEK-ALIGN-1 (dated 2026-08-24) that value is now always the training-week BOUNDARY (day after the runner's long-run day), not the actual visible first day the runner sees. For the common case of a Sunday long run, the boundary is always Monday, so the guard's `getUTCDay() !== 1` check is always false and `frontLoadFirstRun` **never runs at all**, regardless of what day anyone actually onboards on — not just the two narrower no-op cases already named. For other long-run days the guard does fire, but targets the boundary's weekday, not today's, so it can "fix" a day that gets clipped away as pre-signup while the real day-one slot is untouched. Confirmed via `_audit_placement.test.ts` still exercising the pre-WEEK-ALIGN-1 contract (passing the literal onboarding day directly) rather than the boundary-snapped value production now supplies — no test currently proves this didn't reopen. Code-trace only, no executed failing fixture produced. | Implemented, both causes fixed (branch `fix/f063-dayone-doseless-plan-2026-09-14`). Cause A: `OnboardingRevealPanel`'s dose dash was rendered via `.text(_:color:)` sugar, leaving it at `V5.fault`'s off-panel red (measured, contrast-failing on all six ramps) instead of the panel's own ink — fixed to call `FaffValueText` directly with `mark`/`fault` pinned to `V5.OnPanel`, matching `HeroDayPanelContentV5`/`BlockV5.swift`/`RacesV5.swift`. Cause B: `frontLoadFirstRun`'s guard read `startMondayISO` (always the WEEK-ALIGN-1 boundary) instead of the runner's actual first day — fixed via a new optional `ComposePlanInput.firstOwnedDayISO`, wired only at the real onboarding call site, byte-identical for every other caller (self-caught a near-miss mid-implementation where a naive fallback would have silently disabled the mitigation for non-migrated callers — caught by the full-suite digest check, fixed by running the old guard verbatim in the fallback branch). Rule 18 falsified for both causes. **CONFIRMED — `RR-20260914-032`, external review, same exact SHA.** Cause A: confirmed `V5.OnPanel.mark`/`.fault` are real pre-existing tokens (traced `RacesV5.swift`'s genuine use of the same pattern directly, not taken on the diff's own claim); the new falsification test measures real contrast ratios for both old and new tokens every run, no revert-to-check needed. Cause B: read the full `generate.ts` diff directly — the `firstOwnedDayISO` else-branch is a byte-identical copy of the old guard, confirmed the 8,781-archetype corpus stays byte-for-byte preserved (ran that test directly), confirmed `blockStartISO` really is `requestedBlockStartISO(...)`'s result at the cited line. `tsc` clean, Faff scheme build clean. One non-blocking discrepancy flagged rather than waved through: manifest claims 23/23 on the three TS test files, reviewer got 19/19 running them directly — every individually-named closure-criterion test is present and passing, so nothing's actually missing, just a stale count somewhere in the manifest. | F065 | Medium (real, render-confirmed on Watch via the app's own real-engine simulation harness, not a fixture) | Watch stride board shows "STRIDE 3 OF 6" as a huge headline AND "Rep 3 of 6" as a smaller line directly underneath — the same count stated twice, in two words, on the watch's tightest screen. Render-confirmed via `_SessionSim.swift` (`-sim <archetype> -at <seconds>`), the app's own built-in harness mounting the real `WatchRunSurfaceV5` driven by the real engine, not a fixture. `WatchRouterV5.swift`'s `phaseBoard()`/`phaseName()`/`phaseContext()` looks like the current, doctrine-compliant path — a sibling component (`PhaseFaceV6`) has its own comment describing fixing this exact redundancy shape once already ("removing the name entirely is what actually fixed it") — but what actually rendered doesn't match what that path should produce. Two real, unresolved possibilities, honestly flagged rather than guessed at: a literal `phase.label` field bypassing the router's own name logic, or a still-reachable pre-V6 board. Root cause not yet pinned down. Separately: reused the same harness against David's known 2026-09-02 recovery-dead-end plan shape (`-sim recovery-end -warp 60`) — ran through to overtime cleanly, no stuck state, consistent with that fix holding (though the transitional recovery board itself wasn't caught before the warp passed it, so not a full confirmation of that specific board). **Root cause now fully pinned down** (full read of the board view files first, ruling out the persistent-phase-board path): `FacesPhaseV5.swift`/`FacesRunV6.swift` confirmed genuinely clean — phase name/metric role are accessibility-only BY CONSTRUCTION, never a visible `Text`. The real redundancy is a DIFFERENT board — the transition MOMENT takeover, not the persistent phase board. `WorkoutEngine.swift`'s `advance()` work-phase-entry branch (~line 1749) does `flash(.phase(title: p.label, sub: sub), for: 1.6)` where `p.label` is the phase's own stored label (literally "Stride 3 of 6", confirmed in `_SessionSim.swift`'s builder) and `sub` is independently computed as "Rep 3 of 6" (the `hasBand` branch drops pace and leaves a bare rep-count). Both reach `WMomentPhaseChange` as real, visible Text (38pt `WDisplayWord` + 22pt Text directly below) — not one accessibility-only as originally suspected. A nearby comment (`REPCOUNT-1`) describes fixing a related bug (the rep count used to be computed twice and could disagree) — that fix made the two numbers always agree, which likely made this redundancy harder to notice, but didn't remove the underlying "same fact, two phrasings" problem. | **Implemented** (branch `fix/f065-stride-board-redundancy-2026-09-14`), New `labelAlreadyStatesRepCount()` display-only check (not routing, `DOCTRINE-STRIDES-1` stays intact) suppresses the redundant "Rep N of M" restatement. F120 sweep performed: no other instances found. 5 new tests, Rule 18 falsified. This worktree experienced a real environment collision during verification (see RR's Known limitations, and F143) — final state independently re-verified end to end after settling. **CONFIRMED — `RR-20260914-043`, external review, both diffs read in full, target worktree locked so an independent detached worktree was built instead (same practice as RR-042).** Independently confirmed the "reuses the existing recovery-entry nil precedent" doctrine claim by grepping the actual precedent rather than accepting it on faith. Built and tested on a fresh, dedicated, contention-free simulator: 29/29 new-test class, 239/239 full suite, both confirmed via non-zero `totalTestCount`. Rule 18 falsification reproduced the exact predicted signature: reverted the sub-computation gate to pre-fix logic, got exactly 26/29 passing with the 3 predicted redundant-label tests failing with the exact predicted failure text, 2 non-regression tests unaffected, restored, blob hash matched HEAD. Also independently reran `check-watch.sh` against a freshly-booted copy of the pre-approved DC794E30 simulator, reproduced its exact "WATCH-GATE: OK — 239 test cases, 22 boards inside content box" line verbatim. Fully checked, fixed, and independently verified — qualifies for the standing merge authorization. | `8b595fbd36a889d490809b1b64fb2610e7463311` | `for external review/review-requests/RR-20260914-043/` · `for external review/review-results/RR-20260914-043/review.json` · `for design review/findings/2026-09-14-011`, root-cause trace in `SETTINGS-AUDIT-2026-09-14.md` |

| F066 | **SPLIT AND RESOLVED into two separate outcomes — racebail CLEARED (confirmed working), thresholdbail CONFIRMED REAL AND STILL BROKEN.** Finding 022, clean re-test per the agreed plan: same approved watch UDID rebooted (`simctl shutdown`+`boot`, no new device/permission), FaffWatch rebuilt fresh from current source (the on-disk `.app` predated this by 3 weeks), both archetypes re-run with the same ~110s polling rigor as the original test, well past the mile-10 gate and 120s sustain window. **Racebail: FIXED.** Bail board fired correctly — "MILE 10 HEART RATE OVER 163 / The A goal is gone from here · run the B plan..." with Cut it short / Run it out — confirming the device-state confound (the stale Motion & Fitness dialog) fully explains the original failure; the mechanism itself works. **Thresholdbail: STILL BROKEN**, same device, same rebuild, same session — HR mocked 180-191bpm the whole window against a 173 threshold, bail board never appeared, workout ran deep into overtime (34mi on an 8.5mi interval session), identical to the original test. This is the clean split result the pending re-test was designed to produce: proves the honest downgrade was correct (racebail's mechanism was never broken, the test setup was) AND proves thresholdbail has a real, distinct, separately-caused bug that survives the exact fix that cleared racebail. The two archetypes diverge exactly where their code does — racebail is mile-gated (`scope: "mile-10"`, `switch_to_b_goal`), thresholdbail isn't (`scope: "work"`, `drop_to_easy`); since the shared sustain-accumulation machinery (`ruleScopeReached`, `ruleBreachSec`, `shouldOfferBailNow`, `offerBailIfDue`) is now proven working end-to-end via racebail, the bug is most likely specific to the work-scope/`drop_to_easy` path, not the shared core. | **Device-level closed (Rule 13 satisfied) — both archetypes confirmed working on real hardware/simulator, pending external review before merge.** Design review rebuilt FaffWatch fresh from the working tree (confirmed the fix's presence via `git diff` first), same approved rebooted UDID, ran `-sim thresholdbail -warp 60 -hr 185` with the same ~110s polling rigor as the split test — well past the 120s sustain window. Bail board fired correctly: "HEART RATE OVER 173 AND STILL C.../ The stimulus is already banked · forcing the rest of the reps buys fatigue, not fitness." with Cut it short / Run it out. Racebail was already confirmed working. Both archetypes now device-confirmed. `legacy/native/Faff/FaffWatch Watch App/WorkoutEngine.swift`, `tick()`'s `planComplete` early-return branch (was lines 1073-1079): `noteRuleMetric()` — the function that accumulates `ruleBreachSec`, which `shouldOfferBailNow` reads — was only ever called *after* that early return, so the instant a workout's phases finish (`planComplete = true`), every later tick short-circuits before reaching it and `ruleBreachSec` freezes. Shared-core bug, exposed differently by scale: racebail's real-time-anchored ~197s test window closes the 120s HR-sustain check *before* `planComplete` fires, so it never hits the freeze; thresholdbail's distance-anchored reps (`repUnit:.distance`) complete via a **fixed-rate mock accrual** in `WorkoutTracker.swift`'s `startSimulatorMock()` (`0.0045 * warp`, unrelated to the rep's own target pace) that finishes the whole set in ≈39 real seconds under warp — well under the 120s sustain window — so `planComplete` trips and freezes `ruleBreachSec` at ~39, permanently below the 120s threshold; the board can never fire and the session runs into overtime (the 34mi-on-8.5mi overrun). `ruleScopeReached`/`ruleScopeFromMile` and the `action` field (`drop_to_easy` vs `switch_to_b_goal`) were both traced and ruled out as the divergence — scope trivially returns true for non-`mile-N` scopes, and `action` isn't read anywhere in the Watch engine/router (display-only). **Fix**: moved the `noteRuleMetric` call into the `planComplete` branch too (~8 lines, comment cites F066) — extends *when* the existing check runs, changes no threshold/comparison, can't affect racebail's confirmed-working path (unreached there) or double-fire (`bailAnswered`/`pendingQuestion` guards intact). `xcodebuild ... -destination 'generic/platform=watchOS Simulator'` build succeeded, zero errors, no simulator booted per instruction (host was CPU-oversubscribed). Secondary harness-only observation logged separately as F088. | — | `programme-internal-working/00-master-programme/F066-RACE-BAIL-INVESTIGATION-2026-09-14.md`; split confirmed at design review finding 022; device-closed at finding 022 update; fix in working tree at `legacy/native/Faff/FaffWatch Watch App/WorkoutEngine.swift` |
| F066-ORIGINAL-CONTEXT (kept for history, superseded by the row above) | Real instrumentation (NSLog probes at every named link — `bailRule`, `ruleScopeReached`, `noteRuleMetric`, `shouldOfferBailNow`, `offerBailIfDue`, the `.onChange(of: engine.ruleBreachSec)` observer, `canOfferBail` — captured live via `simctl spawn log stream`, verified by actual screenshot per Rule 13) confirms the ENTIRE chain fires correctly: `ruleScopeReached` flips true exactly at mile 10, `ruleBreachSec` climbs 1-for-1 with wall clock, `shouldOfferBailNow` flips true at exactly 120s, `offerBailIfDue` FIRES and sets `pendingQuestion` correctly. Both named hypotheses ruled out with live evidence: nothing else occupies `pendingQuestion` (no routing conflict), and the `-hr` mock is confirmed to reach the same `tracker.heartRate` the real engine reads (no mock-injection gap). **The actual cause found**: a stale, leftover-from-a-different-build native watchOS "Motion & Fitness — Don't Allow" SYSTEM permission sheet was covering the entire screen at the exact moment of firing — an OS-level modal, invisible to and unroutable by the app's own `WatchRouterV5` interrupt-priority model since it isn't a `WInterrupt` the app can see or route around. Confirmed stale (its copy text didn't match the installed bundle's `Info.plist`), confirmed `simctl privacy grant motion` does not clear it, confirmed dismissing it by hand and re-running the IDENTICAL harness produces the correct `FaceBailOfferedV5` board verbatim ("MILE 10 HEART RATE OVER 163" / "Cut it short" / "Run it out"). **Real, durable, genuinely open architectural question this surfaces, NOT yet verified on real hardware**: if motion/fitness authorization is ever genuinely unresolved at race time on a real watch (not just this shared-simulator artifact), the same invisible-OS-modal-blocking-a-safety-board failure mode could occur for real, with no mechanism today for the app to detect or route around an OS-level permission sheet blocking safety UI. No fix implemented — correctly left as a real, deliberate architectural decision rather than rushed, given the safety stakes. **Reconciliation done, answered precisely and honestly, not defensively.** Design review confirmed both their racebail and thresholdbail tests ran on the IDENTICAL simulator device (no reboot between them) — a real shared variable they hadn't flagged when originally reporting "confidence raised by a second independent reproduction." They did NOT personally observe a Motion & Fitness dialog in any of ~15 screenshots across both tests, but their sampling was periodic (every 10-60+ seconds), not continuous — a transient dialog appearing and clearing between two captures would be invisible to them. **Honest, correct conclusion: this is no longer "two independent confirmations of a broken mechanism" — it's two failed reproductions on one device whose permission state was never directly verified, consistent with either a genuinely broken mechanism or the same environmental artifact.** Their own evidence doesn't distinguish between the two. Proposed next step, agreed: a clean re-test on the SAME already-approved device after a full reboot (`simctl shutdown` + `boot` on the existing UDID — not a new device, doesn't need a new permission grant) rather than continuing to debate the existing screenshots. | Open pending one clean re-test (reboot the existing approved device, don't mint a new one, re-run both racebail and thresholdbail); real root cause for the ORIGINAL racebail failure is still confirmed (the stale dialog was directly observed and fixed by dismissing it) — what's unresolved is only whether thresholdbail's separate failure shares that cause | — | `programme-internal-working/00-master-programme/F066-RACE-BAIL-INVESTIGATION-2026-09-14.md` |

**Second, independent reproduction confirms this is NOT one test's quirk.** `thresholdbail` (`-sim thresholdbail -warp 60 -hr 185`) is a structurally different rule from racebail — different kind ("bail" not "abort"), different action ("drop_to_easy" not "switch_to_b_goal"), different scope (ungated "work", not mile-10-gated), different threshold (173 vs 163), different workout shape (a short 4x1mi interval session, not a marathon). Same result: HR mocked ~190bpm continuously from launch through warmup and all four intervals, bail board never appeared, the whole 8.5mi workout ran to completion and into overtime with nothing ever offered. Two independent rule instances, different kind/scope/threshold/workout, both failing under conditions each one's own code says should trigger — much harder to explain as a test-setup artifact, much more consistent with a real break in the SHARED machinery both paths route through (`noteRuleMetric` → `ruleBreachSec` → `shouldOfferBailNow` → `offerBailIfDue`). | Open — urgent, confidence raised by cross-check; code agent already dispatched with runtime instrumentation, explicitly told not to just re-read source again | — | `for design review/findings/2026-09-14-012` |

| F078 | Medium (real, deterministic, precisely diagnosed, small mechanical fix) | Design review render-confirmed (synthetic week-off row, local scratch DB only, reverted immediately): wire data for a week-off range is `fromISO`/`toISO` both "2026-09-14," but the rendered screen shows "September 13 – 13" — a full day behind on both ends. Root cause: `WeekOffV5.formatRange()` correctly parses the ISO string with `timeZone=UTC`, but builds the displayed string with a fresh `Calendar` and two `DateFormatter`s that never get a timezone set — they silently default to device-local, behind UTC for the whole US, so UTC midnight renders as the prior evening. Confirmed isolated, not a pattern: `RunLogV5.swift`'s date formatters were checked for comparison and correctly set `timeZone=UTC` on both ends. For a real multi-day travel block both ends shift together, so the range still looks internally coherent (e.g. "Sep 30 – Oct 6" renders "Sep 29 – Oct 5") — easy for a runner to not notice, which is why pinning down the exact mechanism mattered rather than leaving it as "looks a bit off." Fix: three missing `timeZone` assignments to match the parsing stage's own already-correct pattern. | Implemented (branch `fix/f078-weekoff-timezone-2026-09-14`). | F082 | Medium (real, root cause found, fix implemented and verified against real data — pending simulator render + external review) | Design review, RaceDetailV5's Pace Plan section shows two nonsensical zero-width segment ranges — "MILES 2-2" and "MILES 6-6" — alongside normal ranges. Render-confirmed on David's real account, real race (Dodgers 10K, Sep 26). **Root cause confirmed**: `web-v2/app/api/v5/race/[slug]/route.ts` rounded each pace-plan phase's mile label independently (`Math.round(start)-Math.round(end)`) with no continuity check — Dodgers' real course-elevation "Descent" phase (1.77mi→2.486mi) both round to whole-mile 2, collapsing to "Miles 2-2." Generic to any course whose elevation phases don't land on whole-mile boundaries, not race-specific. **Fix implemented**: phases that would collapse on the mile grid are merged into an adjacent real phase (time-weighted pace blend, label from whichever phase covers more ground) before display; the underlying pace math/goal-time drift check still runs against the unmerged phases — display-only fix. Verified by running the real `buildRacePacing` code against real DB course geometry for all 4 scheduled races: only Dodgers had the bug (before: `0-2\|2-2\|2-3\|3-4\|4-6\|6-6`, after: `0-2\|2-3\|3-4\|4-6`); Malibu, CIM, LA Marathon unaffected before and after. `tsc --noEmit` and full `next build` both pass. **Committed** at `23e1f9dd4` on `fix/f082-race-pace-segment-labels-2026-09-14-v2`, not yet merged. **Partial render confirmation**: design review got a genuine (if capped) render window — RaceDetailV5 for Dodgers confirmed rendering correctly end-to-end today, hero card/Goal-Evidence panel/course elevation chart all displaying real data with no errors, before the simulator's touch-input degradation (F116) stopped scrolling from working. Could not scroll far enough to reach the Pace Plan section itself to confirm the specific mile-range-label fix — honestly reported as partial, not claimed as full. **Full render confirmation, 2026-09-15**: F116's render environment cleared; re-rendered the same screen against David's real account copy, all four Pace Plan segments now read as real spans (MILES 0-2, 2-3, 3-4, 4-6), no zero-width range. RESOLVED. | Closed — implemented, data-verified, and now fully render-confirmed end to end. Still needs submission through external review before merge (not yet dispatched separately — will queue alongside other unmerged fixes). | `23e1f9dd4` | `for design review/findings/2026-09-14-016`; fix committed at `web-v2/app/api/v5/race/[slug]/route.ts` |

| F083 | High (real, confirmed unreachable content behind a broken affordance) | Design review: Settings → Coach → "Decisions" screen genuinely does not scroll — 4 different gesture techniques tried (2 swipe variants + a 5-point `touch_path` drag), zero movement, while the identical swipe technique scrolled Today/Block/Races/RaceDetailV5 fine the same session. Result: the last SETTLED card's body text is cut off mid-sentence behind the tab bar and is permanently unreadable on a screen whose whole point is showing the full record of coach decisions. Same screen also shows "Holding the plan as it is" TWICE with byte-identical text on the same day — once as HOLD/WAITING ON YOU (open), once as HOLD/EXPIRED (settled) — can't tell from the render alone whether that's a state-machine duplicate-write bug or an unlabeled intentional daily-recurrence pattern. | Open — needs the code agent to trace both halves: (1) why this screen's scroll container doesn't respond to any gesture that works elsewhere, (2) whether the duplicate HOLD card is a duplicate-write bug or a real recurrence the UI should label as such (see also F060, the same duplicate-card question logged separately). **Narrowed, 2026-09-15, not solved but ruled OUT in specific ways — design review read `DecisionHistoryV5.swift` (315 lines) in full since the screen couldn't be reached by render tonight.** Defect 1 (non-scroll): source is structurally clean — one `ScrollView` wrapping the whole body, no nested-scroll conflict like `INJURYCHECKIN-1` elsewhere in this file family, no fixed-height trap, and `DecisionHistoryHostV5` (`HostsV5.swift:4318`) doesn't add another scroll layer either. Not an obvious missing-`ScrollView` bug — whatever's actually stopping the gesture isn't visible in this file's layout code. **Possible shared root cause flagged, not asserted**: F085 (finding 020, app-wide input lag, currently downgraded to low/environmental) and F135 (this same screen's "Decisions" row refusing to navigate at all tonight) may be three symptoms of one cause rather than three separate things — worth checking together before chasing each independently. Defect 2 (duplicate card): confirmed a genuine backend/data question, not a view bug — `groups()` splits purely on the `outcome` field values the server sends; if two rows with identical content but different `outcome` values exist in the underlying array, this view has no way to know they're "the same decision" and renders them exactly as observed. Narrows the code agent's first guess away from the render layer entirely for defect 2. **Correction, 2026-09-15: F060's own CONFIRMED resolution shows the mechanism was slightly different than this narrowing suggested.** It's not "two rows the view can't tell apart" in the data-duplication sense — it's two GENUINELY DIFFERENT decision events (different raised dates, different underlying workouts) that happen to render byte-identically because the template omits a field (`dateISO`) that was already sitting unconditionally on the wire the whole time. Data was fine; the fix ended up being presentation-layer (F060's subtitle addition), not a backend trace. Worth knowing before assuming F083's own duplicate-card half needs backend work — it may not, if it's the same underlying mechanism as F060 rather than a genuinely distinct data-integrity case. **Live reconfirmation, 2026-09-15 — both original defects still present on real data, once render access was actually reached.** Defect 2 (duplicate card): "Holding the plan as it is" still appears verbatim in both STILL OPEN and SETTLED cards (Sep 14 dates this time, same shape). Defect 1 (won't scroll): still present, confirmed via two separate swipe gestures at different distances, zero pixel movement, content still cut off at the bottom (different exact text than the original report, same failure shape). **New, possibly severity-relevant: after this render, the ENTIRE screen stopped responding — not just scrolling.** Back button, tab bar taps, nothing worked across multiple attempts and a 4-second wait (ruling out simple input-lag rather than assuming a hard stop). Host load was oscillating during the attempt (239→291) so load contamination can't be fully ruled out — flagged honestly rather than overclaimed. But this is now the FOURTH time tonight this specific screen has failed to respond to further interaction after its initial render, across genuinely different load conditions each time — a real pattern starting to look screen-specific rather than coincidental generic contention, the same shape F134 found for `/api/settings` (one specific surface failing repeatedly while everything else recovers). Recovered by terminating the app. **Cross-finding comparison, design review, 2026-09-15, finding 045: NOT shown to share F134's cause, despite the surface-level resemblance.** F083 freezes AFTER a successful render — not a fetch-failure story like F134/F093's shared `/api/settings` breakage, and F083 never touches that route at all. Grouped instead with F146 (Travel sheet, also render-then-freeze, also render-confirmed multiple times) as a second, distinct cluster — real, but with cause not yet found for either. **One confound flagged honestly, not ruled out: F020 (app-wide input lag from earlier tonight) is a live third explanation for this whole cluster's symptoms** — a queued tap firing seconds late is indistinguishable from a genuinely dead tap under black-box testing alone. Reviewer's own conclusion: this cluster is past what more interaction-testing can resolve from the outside; needs the same instrumented build already set up for F137, not another render pass. Open — this screen (`DecisionHistoryV5`) is accumulating a real pattern of its own beyond the two originally-reported defects, now grouped with F146 as "Cluster 2" pending instrumented investigation (not black-box render passes) to separate a genuine UI freeze from F020 input-lag masquerading as one. | — | Cross-session message, design review, 2026-09-14 (Settings audit, finding 018; the session's own local finding 016 is F082 above, 017 is the capped-confidence precursor to F085 below); narrowing read, `for design review/SETTINGS-AUDIT-2026-09-14.md`, 2026-09-15; corrected against F060's confirmed resolution; live reconfirmation + 4th-freeze pattern, 2026-09-15; cross-finding cluster comparison (finding 045), design review, 2026-09-15 |

| F092 | Medium (real doctrine-conformance gap, needs a product decision — not urgent, not a safety or data-integrity issue) | Independent product review (IPR-20260914-009): traced every notification trigger against `docs/PRODUCT_ROADMAP.md` Priority 4's explicit rule ("notifications are reserved for consequential changes or requested actions"). 6 of ~9 categories fit cleanly (`session_moved`, `projection_change`, `strava_reconnect`, `skip_recovery`, `niggle_sick`; `race_day`/`race_eve` borderline but time-driven-consequential). Real counterargument worth weighing: `weekly_checkin`/`race_countdown` may be a deliberate, already-shipped product choice (the design contract's own "Weekly summary" toggle suggests intent) that simply postdates Priority 4's wording and was never reconciled against it, rather than an unnoticed violation — `run_unread` is the hardest of the three to defend under that reading. | **RESOLVED — David's ruling, direct, via David's Desk (2026-09-14 night), verbatim: "Leave them as deliberate exceptions."** All three stay on with no Settings toggle, treated as an intentional carve-out from the consequential-changes-only notification rule. | — | `for independent product review/reports/IPR-20260914-009`; ruling via David's Desk |
| F093 | High (real, confirmed regression against the legacy screen's own correct behavior, not a gap that was never built) | Design review, finding 023, source-confirmed (`SettingsV5.swift` → `onSetLongRunDay` → `patch(["long_run_day": ...])` → `PATCH /api/settings`): the server explicitly treats `long_run_day` as `PLAN_SHAPING` and calls `rebuildActivePlanForPrefs`, which regenerates the active plan around the new day and returns a `replanned` boolean — but the V5 client's `patch()` wrapper discards that response entirely (`_ = try await write(); return true`), so no code path in `SettingsV5`/`HostsV5` ever reads `replanned`. Confirmed as a regression, not a never-built gap: the legacy pre-V5 `SettingsView.swift` handles the identical field correctly and shows a "Plan updated" toast on the same flag. Net effect: a runner moves their long run from Sunday to Saturday, the app silently reshuffles the week's quality/easy placement around it, and nothing on screen tells them that happened. Scope confirmed narrow, not broader: a full inventory of every `onSet*`/`onToggle*` closure in `SettingsV5.swift`+`HostsV5.swift` found only 4 phone-editable fields total (`long_run_day`, `units_distance`, `phone_run_enabled`, `weekly_frequency`), and only `long_run_day` is plan-shaping — `rest_day`/`quality_days`/`available_days` have no phone control yet at all, so they don't share this bug today (worth the same fix whenever they get UI). Distance units confirmed clean by the same reviewer — display-only, no rebuild triggered. **Render-confirmed (Rule 13) 2026-09-15, real device, with the exact mechanism identified, not just the symptom re-observed.** Changed Long run day Sunday→Wednesday: the row updated optimistically to Wednesday immediately, then silently reverted to Sunday seconds later, zero toast/banner/acknowledgment either direction. Same shape recurs at every rung boundary, always downward, up to ~10mi off at the worst case. This is every new runner's first plan, at the exact moment their trust in the app is being formed. Needs a product decision, not just a code fix: either make the stepper only offer the real rungs (no illusion of granularity), or show the resolved/snapped value back before the runner confirms. Positive findings from the same pass, worth keeping on record: no Rule 17 duplicate sentences anywhere in onboarding, coach-voice/tone fully clean (no exclamation marks, no emoji, em dashes only in dev comments), and the reveal panel correctly reads live Today data rather than composing its own preview (avoiding a Rule 16 trap). | **David widened this rather than picking either option — direct, via David's Desk (2026-09-15), verbatim: "This whole thing seems broken. Why would the default be 24 but they get 15? We should never ask a runner to pick a number and not give it to them. Other agents need to weigh in here because this isn't a simple answer it seems like a broken system."** Now standing doctrine, `CLAUDE.md` Rule 25. Two things need real investigation before any fix: (1) why the stepper's own DEFAULT (24) resolves off-rung in the first place — may be a deeper bug than the rounding-on-confirm behavior originally described; (2) whether the same "ask for one number, silently substitute another" shape appears anywhere else in onboarding or the app — not assumed, not yet checked. **Investigation complete, both questions traced with direct evidence.** (1) Why the default (24) is off-rung: `OnboardingV5.swift`'s own comment (lines 653-654) says exactly where it came from — "Lifted from the prototype's own `_script-data.js`... the `onboard.weeklyMi` default of 24" — a design-prototype placeholder migrated verbatim into production, never checked against `VALID_WEEKLY_MI` at all. Not an edge case slipping through a correct rule — the default was never checked against the rule in the first place. (2) Whether this pattern is systemic: **yes, confirmed — this is the SECOND time this exact class of bug has hit this same screen.** `submit()`'s own header comment documents a 2026-08-21 onboarding audit that found and fixed 4 instances of "client control range/precision silently disagreeing with what the server validates": name never sent (fixed), `weeklyFreq` off a 2-7 stepper vs. `VALID_FREQ`'s 6-ceiling (fixed — stepper is now 2-6, exact match, confirmed by direct comparison — this is the CORRECT template), `longRunDay` landing too late to affect the first plan build (fixed), and `weeklyMi` itself — the August audit's own words: "weeklyMi was sent unconditionally from a 10...70 stepper... so 24 was dropped, AND it was sent for runners who never answered a volume question at all." That was only HALF-fixed: the audit gated it correctly and added `snapWeeklyMi` to guarantee a valid rung — but "round down, say nothing" is itself a fresh instance of the identical defect class, just relabeled from "silently dropped" to "silently substituted" without the August audit recognizing it as the same bug shape. Mechanically, the specific snap-to-rung table is isolated (grepped all of `ViewsV5`, it's the only one) — but the general class (a client control's range/precision disagreeing with what the route actually validates) has now produced 5 known instances on one screen across two investigations three weeks apart. | **Fix authorized by Rule 25 — real rungs only, matching `daysPerWeek`'s already-correct pattern (its 2-6 stepper range exactly matches `VALID_FREQ`, making its downstream clamp pure defensive redundancy that can never trigger).** Dispatched to code agent. Given this is a confirmed-repeat bug class (5 instances, 2 investigations), also worth a standing check — verify every client input control's range/precision against its actual route validator, not just patch this one field. **David's second question — does this shape appear elsewhere — now answered, not just checked.** Independent product review ran a full Rule 25 census (23 controls, Onboarding + Today/Block, UI-to-payload traced against each real server validator): 16 clean, 5 already-known (this finding, F074), 1 genuinely new and narrow (F129, a timezone extraction bug in the travel date picker). **Confirmed NOT systemic** — this pattern is not a widespread defect class in these two screens, it's two specific mechanisms. | — | `for design review/SETTINGS-AUDIT-2026-09-14.md` (finding 028, full investigation); David's widened ruling via David's Desk, 2026-09-15; Rule 25 census confirming non-systemic, `for external review` independent product review, 2026-09-15 |

| F110 | **High — SAFETY, same class as F066, identical bug shape one level up in the same file** | Design review, finding 032, full read of `WorkoutEngine.swift` (the same file F066's fix lives in). `noteMileBand(inBand:)` is the ONLY call site that updates `milesAdrift` (confirmed via grep), and `milesAdrift >= 2` is the sole trigger for a `metric: "pace"` bail. But `noteMileBand` only fires when `allowSplitFlash` is true — and `allowSplitFlash` is explicitly false during exactly the multi-rep structured work (interval/threshold/tempo), by design, to avoid interrupting a quality rep with a split-flash board. So a mile crossed MID-REP during a 2-3mi threshold/tempo work phase never reaches `noteMileBand` — mile bookkeeping still advances correctly (`lastMileIndex`/`mileSplits`), but the drift counter freezes. Worse: outside work phases `paceZone` is always `.onTarget`, so any crossing during recovery/warmup that DOES reach `noteMileBand` resets `milesAdrift` TOWARD ZERO — actively working against the rare crossings that do count. Net: for exactly the sessions a pace-based bail exists to protect (can't hold target pace rep after rep), the counter needed to ever reach 2 is starved. **Same lesson as F066** — a display-suppression branch (`allowSplitFlash`) accidentally also gating safety-relevant accounting, not the specific `planComplete` branch this time but the identical shape. Not yet render-verified — no existing `_SessionSim.swift` archetype pairs a `metric:"pace"` bail with a multi-mile work rep (closest is "threshold", 2x3mi work reps, but zero rules attached); reviewer correctly did not write a new test archetype themselves (product/test code, outside their remit), precise repro recipe is in the finding file. Drift-warning board appeared correctly mid-rep, then the bail fired: "TWO MILES ADRIFT / The pace has not landed for two miles running · drop to easy and bank what the session already gave you." — required `milesAdrift` to accumulate across mid-rep mile crossings inside a live 3-mile tempo work rep, exactly the starved scenario this finding identified. | F114 | Low-Medium (infrastructure/tooling integrity, not app code — but explains real observed flakiness tonight and could produce a false "passed" on a genuinely broken change if unlucky) | Code-agent lead, live during F056/F057's merge-verification: running `xcodebuild test` against the SAME booted simulator (`Faff-Review-1`) from two independent worktree processes concurrently produces cross-contaminated results — a test run scoped to one worktree's `FaffTests` target reported a test case (`InjuryFlareTitleWiringTests`, from a completely different worktree's F075 branch, confirmed absent from the reporting worktree's own git history and source tree) as running and failing. Confirmed not a DerivedData-folder collision (each worktree's DerivedData is correctly isolated by path, verified via `info.plist`'s `WorkspacePath`) — the contamination happens at the shared simulator's XCTest daemon/test-manager level when two `xcodebuild test` processes target the identical simulator UDID at once. Reproduced the mechanism directly: identical test invocation against the SAME worktree gave TEST FAILED (with the phantom cross-worktree test case), then TEST SUCCEEDED, then TEST FAILED again, purely as a function of whether another agent's concurrent Xcode test run was active against the same simulator at that moment — resolved cleanly (multiple consecutive clean runs) once confirmed no other `xcodebuild`/`xctest` process was running. F075's own agent independently and separately reported "the simulator turned out to be under concurrent use by another session (confirmed via device logs)" during its own verification pass the same night — independent corroboration, not a single observer's guess. **Retrospective concern, not yet investigated**: some of tonight's earlier "flaky, resolved by retry" observations (e.g. the watch-gate board-render flake noted in F063/F074's evidence) may share this same root mechanism rather than being Railway-side or render-timing flakiness as assumed at the time — worth a look, not urgent, since retries did resolve them and no wrong verdict is known to have shipped. **Real risk this poses going forward**: a genuinely broken change could show a clean "TEST SUCCEEDED" if a concurrent process's passing results happen to paper over the real failure, or (less likely but possible) a genuinely correct change could show a false failure, in either direction depending on timing luck — a merge/RR-submission decision made from a single xcodebuild run during a period of known concurrent Xcode/simulator activity should not be fully trusted without a confirming re-run once contention is cleared. | Open, low urgency — worth a standing rule addition (check `ps aux \| grep xcodebuild` / `xcrun simctl list devices booted` for contention before trusting a single native test run's verdict, especially right before a merge decision) and, longer-term, worth exploring whether per-worktree simulator assignment or explicit `-derivedDataPath` isolation actually prevents the daemon-level cross-talk (untested — this finding only diagnoses the mechanism, doesn't yet confirm a structural fix) | — | Code-agent lead, live discovery during F056/F057 merge processing, 2026-09-14/15; cross-referenced independently by F075's own implementer report (`programme-internal-working/00-master-programme/F075-INJURY-SEVERITY-TITLE-WIRING-2026-09-14.md`); another live instance during `RR-20260914-040` review, 2026-09-15 — a simulator silently died mid-run (0.0% CPU, same signature as this session's own earlier RR-028/RR-030 hang diagnosis), stopped via `TaskStop`, fresh boot recovered cleanly. Confirms this is a real, recurring shared-machine problem rather than a one-off, not specific to any single unit under test; a third instance during `RR-20260914-041`'s review (minutes after the second, on `RR-040`), same 0%-CPU hang signature, same `TaskStop`-and-retry resolution — three instances now across two consecutive review units, strengthening the case this is a real, current shared-machine load problem rather than a rare fluke; a fourth, more precisely-characterized instance during `RR-20260914-042`'s review — a disabled-guard hang inside real HealthKit calls confirmed via CPU-time-vs-wall-clock polling (0.21s CPU over 180s wall-clock) rather than assumed from a stuck screenshot, resolved by killing only the exact captured PID rather than a broad pkill (the safer alternative to a self-disclosed known_limitations concern in the reviewed RR) |

| F119 | **High — the exact mechanism meant to prevent this file's own named incident ("a recorded run once vanished") reaches nobody today** | Design review, finding 035, full 1040-line read of `PhoneSync.swift` (the watch-to-phone/server run-completion bridge). **Finding 1, the standout**: `syncState` and `lastSyncError` are both documented in-file as existing specifically to tell a runner "your run didn't sync" — neither has a reader anywhere in the app (grepped both exact property names repo-wide, zero hits outside this file). A fully-saved run would show permanently-wrong failed status if anything read it — latent today only because of Finding 1. **Finding 4**: the durable retry queue silently evicts its oldest entries past 50, no error set, no log — only triggers after 50 runs' worth of compounding failure, but that's the exact scenario the file's own header says already happened once. **Finding 2, plausible not confirmed**: an encode failure in `sendCompletion` may drop a run before it even reaches the retry queue, no trace at all — not yet traced whether NaN/Infinity is actually reachable from real GPS/duration math. **Finding 5, minor**: 2 discarded WCSession writes (same shape as tonight's other findings), governs a handshake not the run data itself, lower severity. Credit where due: the underlying design is genuinely strong — dual-path completion (WC transfer + durable-queue POST surviving suspension) mirrors this project's own `V5WriteSettlement` pattern, `apply()`'s merge is correctly field-level not full-replace (Rule 6 compliant), no display-suppression-gates-accumulation shape anywhere in this file. The zero-reader claim holds — but `syncState`/`lastSyncError` hits DO appear elsewhere in a repo-wide grep, and each was individually chased down rather than assumed innocent: `ios/Runcino/Views/PlanView.swift`'s own unrelated local `@State syncState` (coincidental name, different old codebase); `PlanSnapshotStore.swift`'s own distinct `syncState`/`lastError` (plan-snapshot sync, not run-completion sync); and the real trap — `RequestDiagnosticsView.swift:94` LOOKS like a reader at first grep, but its binding is confirmed via the type annotation to read `PlanSnapshotStore.SyncState`, not `PhoneSync`'s. No external reader of PhoneSync's actual properties exists anywhere. Safe for implementation. **Design note delivered** (finding 037, full read of `FacesFinishV5.swift` first to ground it in real existing conventions, not invented ones): governing constraint is David's own quoted rule in that file — "there is not done or save or anything button" — the status line must be purely informational, never gates/disables Done. Three states specified: `.sent` → show NOTHING (matches this file's own `FinishCompleteBoard.coachLine` precedent — stating the obvious reads as a brush-off); `.sending` → "Saving", quiet/dim (`WatchV5.valueMute`), no alarm; `.failed(reason)` → "Still saving · trying another way", AMBER not red (this file's own header states the doctrine almost word for word for a different board — "AMBER, NOT RED... nothing here failed to read" — same logic applies, since `.failed` here means the primary transport hiccuped and the durable backup is auto-retrying). Placement: directly above Done, below totals — respects the board's strict first-screenful rule. | **Implemented, but NOT actually submitted for external review despite the code agent's own report saying so** — same packaging gap as F074/`RR-20260914-031`, independently verified rather than trusted this time given the precedent: `python3 "for external review/tools/validate-review-request.py" "for external review/review-requests/RR-20260914-044"` returns INVALID — no READY sentinel, `evidence_paths[0]` points at `programme-internal-management/00-master-programme/F119-PHONESYNC-STATUS-2026-09-14.md` which doesn't exist (same broken-path shape as RR-031), and the branch's own worktree isn't clean (`native-v2/Faff.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` has 5 uncommitted lines, likely benign Xcode churn but unverified). (branch `fix/f119-phonesync-status-2026-09-14`, `RR-20260914-044`, packaging incomplete). All 3 core fixes landed: (1) `FinishSummaryBoard` now draws a real 3-state status line per finding 037's exact spec, after confirming the async-timing caveat as a stale doc comment (`sendCompletion` is fire-and-forget, fires well before Summary renders — corrected in place); (2) one-way guard bug fixed — a late backup-POST success now corrects `syncState` from either `.sending` or `.failed` to `.sent`; (3) retry-queue eviction past 50 now logs the evicted run's id via a new `evictionLogger` seam, no longer silent. | F124 | **HIGH — SAFETY-ADJACENT, escalated with the same weight as F066/F110 tonight: real control loss during an active outdoor run, not a display bug** | Design review, finding 039, full read of `WorkoutRootView.swift` (the app's root state machine, distinct from `WatchRouterV5` which routes within an already-active run). `content`'s top-level gate checks `treadmillHR.isActive` FIRST, unconditionally, before `model.engine` (the live outdoor-run engine). The comment's own reasoning only considered the idle case ("takes precedence over the idle TabView") — nothing addresses what happens when an outdoor run is already active. Traced the other side: `TreadmillHRSession.start()` has no check against any watch-owned run state at all — unconditionally starts a second `HKWorkoutSession` the instant the phone sends `startTreadmillHR`. The existing symmetric guard (`blockedByPhone`/DUPLICATE-1) only protects the opposite direction (watch `launch()` refusing when the phone's Run tab is active) — never extended to this entry point. `TreadmillHRView` has NO Stop/back control at all — its own header comment documents one was deliberately removed in an earlier draft; the view only disappears when the PHONE sends `stopTreadmillHR`. **Concrete failure**: runner starts a watch-tracked outdoor run (Pause/Lap/Skip/End&Save all showing), the phone's TreadmillView gets opened and started (mistake, muscle memory, shared pairing) — watch screen silently swaps to a control-less HR readout. The outdoor run keeps recording in the background (engine/tracker are plain ObservableObjects independent of what's drawn), but the runner has ZERO controls over it — no Pause, no Skip, no End&Save — until the phone-side session ends, entirely outside their control from the wrist. Honestly flagged as unconfirmed: whether watchOS tolerates two concurrent `HKWorkoutSession`s cleanly or whether this compounds into data corruption needs device verification the reviewer can't do from source. Same shape as F120's Pattern 1 (a careful fix on one entry point, never extended to the structural sibling) but applied to the control surface instead of a display value — much higher stakes. The code-agent brief's own §6 is explicit and correct on both counts: copy every evidence artifact into the request's own immutable `evidence/` directory, AND run the worktree-aware validator in `--preflight` mode before creating `READY`. Both bugs found on RR-031/RR-044 are things plain `validate-review-request.py` already catches — `--preflight` would have caught them before `READY` ever existed. Simplest explanation: that step was being skipped, not that the guidance was wrong. **Confirmed via the next batch, not just theorized: of 7 new requests that landed (032, 033, 034, 035, 036, 041, 043), 6 are VALID** — whatever caused the containment issue looks fixed going forward. **Root cause #2, still live, more persistent: `native-v2/Faff.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` specifically, whenever xcodegen regenerates it.** RR-034 fails validation right now — `changed_files does not match git diff`, missing exactly this file — a different failure mode than RR-044's (there it was uncommitted; here it's just missing from the declared list), same file, same trigger (a submission needing xcodegen to register new tests). Reviewer's working theory, not confirmed: a large, low-signal generated file that's easy to drop by habit when someone eyeballs a diff instead of literally running `git diff --name-only <base>..<exact>` as the brief instructs. | **RESOLVED 2026-09-15, code-agent lead.** RR-034 fixed directly (missing `project.pbxproj` added to `changed_files`, confirmed genuine via `git diff --stat <base>..<exact>` -- 9 insertions/0 deletions, a real xcodegen registration, not the usual UUID-churn reorder shape). Went further than the reported scope and independently ran the real validator (not `--preflight` -- the full check, exactly the ordering mistake this finding itself warns against, corrected for future submissions) across every RR from tonight, 031 through 044 -- found 5 MORE instances beyond the 8 already known (RR-037/038/039/040/042, all older findings whose implementing worktrees had since been removed after merging, same evidence-containment root cause). RR-038 and RR-042 also independently found to share Root cause #2 (a genuine pbxproj registration missing from `changed_files`) -- not previously reported for those two. Fixed all 5 the same way: pulled the implementer's report from git history via `git show <sha>:<path>` (worktree gone, but the file is permanently committed on the branch), copied it into the request's own `evidence/` directory, corrected `changed_files`, created `READY`. Verified each via a detached-checkout worktree at the exact SHA (`git worktree add <path> <sha> --detach`) so `worktree_clean` could be independently confirmed rather than skipped. **All 14 requests, RR-031 through RR-044, now confirmed VALID by the real validator.** Adopting the reviewer's process fix going forward: run `--preflight` before creating `READY`, not after. **Independently re-verified by the programme lead, 2026-09-15, not taken on the code agent's report alone given the night's track record**: a bare validator run against all 14 initially showed 5 false negatives (037/038/039/040/042) — not a real defect, just that a plain invocation can't confirm `worktree_clean` once a worktree's been removed. Reproduced the code agent's own method exactly (isolated detached checkout at each request's `exact_git_sha`, `--worktree=<path>` passed to the validator, checkout discarded after) for all 5 — every one came back genuinely `VALID REVIEW REQUEST`. The claim holds. **Third distinct packaging defect class found, 2026-09-15 (RR-20260914-045/F126): the manifest claimed `subject.worktree_clean: true`, but no real worktree existed anywhere on disk for that branch/SHA at review time.** Different shape from both prior classes (evidence-path containment on RR-031/RR-044; a genuinely uncommitted/undeclared pbxproj change on RR-034/RR-044) — this one is a false claim about a worktree that simply doesn't exist. Not blocking (external reviewer built their own independent detached worktree at the exact SHA rather than trust the claim, per standing discipline), but worth root-causing alongside the other two: whatever writes `worktree_clean` into the manifest needs to check a worktree actually exists before asserting anything about its state, not just report a stale/assumed value. **Fourth distinct packaging-state class, 2026-09-15 (RR-20260914-038/F029): dirty-but-harmless.** A pre-existing worktree had an actually-dirty `project.pbxproj` on arrival despite `worktree_clean: true` — but this time the dirt itself was genuinely benign: pure xcodegen regeneration noise (reordered entries, fresh random UUIDs for the same file reference), semantically identical to the committed version. Reviewer correctly inspected the diff directly rather than either trusting the clean claim or blindly discarding, reset via `git checkout` to the exact committed state, confirmed nothing else touched, then validated. Distinct from class 2 (RR-034/044's genuinely-changed-and-undeclared pbxproj) and class 3 (RR-045's claimed-clean-but-no-worktree-exists) — this is claimed-clean, worktree exists, genuinely dirty, but the dirt doesn't matter. Four classes now on the same underlying field (`worktree_clean`/`changed_files` around `project.pbxproj` specifically) — strengthens the case that xcodegen's interaction with this one file is the actual common thread worth fixing at the source, not the manifest-writing step alone. **Fifth instance, `RR-20260914-040`, 2026-09-15**: another dirty-but-harmless `project.pbxproj`, this time from xcodegen auto-discovering the new test file through the `native-v2`/`legacy-native` hard-link mirror specifically — a more precise trigger mechanism than previously named, worth citing directly when this finally gets fixed at the source. **Actual root cause confirmed, 2026-09-15 — not drift, deliberate non-determinism.** Code agent diffed two back-to-back xcodegen regenerations of identical source: it generates fresh random `TEMP_<UUID>` group/file-reference identifiers on EVERY run regardless of content, which is why the noise is always insertions=deletions (pure churn, safe to discard) except when a real new file is added, where the diff carries a net insertion delta — that delta is the actual signal to check for, not the presence of any diff at all. Real-world consequence confirmed the same night: F029 and F060 independently modified the same `pbxproj` text for their own new test files, which git's merge genuinely could not auto-resolve — a real conflict, not noise, fixed by merging sequentially rather than as one octopus merge and re-running xcodegen once at the end to produce one consistent file with both registrations, rather than hand-resolving the generated text. This closes the investigative thread `--preflight`/`validate-review-request.py` were built to catch symptoms of. | — | External reviewer's root-cause investigation, 2026-09-15; register entries F074/F119 for the two originating instances; independently re-verified, 2026-09-15; third defect class via `RR-20260914-045`/F126 review; fourth defect class via `RR-20260914-038`/F029 review |
